- Targets: 645

| `pc-3ec33907d87b` | composite-unique: Columns datasource_name, workspace_id must be unique | `DATABASE_ENFORCED_UNHANDLED` | yes | database | no | unhandled | 0.95 | `ds-control`, `ds-dup-name-same-ws`, `r2-ds-update-rename-dup`, `ds-race-r0-t0`, `ds-race-r0-t1`, `ds-race-r0-t2`, `ds-race-r0-t3`, `ds-race-r0-t4`, `ds-race-r0-t5`, `ds-race-r1-t0`, `ds-race-r1-t1`, `ds-race-r1-t2`, `ds-race-r1-t3`, `ds-race-r1-t4`, `ds-race-r1-t5`, `ds-race-r2-t0`, `ds-race-r2-t1`, `ds-race-r2-t2`, `ds-race-r2-t3`, `ds-race-r2-t4`, `ds-race-r2-t5` | [Columns datasource_name, workspace_id must be unique] Sequential duplicates are rejected by the application pre-check checkDatasourceNameUnique (code 60004 'datasource [..] already exists'), which is scoped to the current workspace. The pre-check is not atomic: under 6 concurrent creates of the same name, several requests passed the SELECT and reached the INSERT, where H2 raised 23505 'Unique index or primary key violation: CONSTRAINT_INDEX_E ON public.t_st_datasource(datasource_name, workspace_id)'. Exactly one row per name persisted; the DB rejection was surfaced as generic code 99999 with the raw SQL message. The DB is therefore the actual guarantee and its rejection is unhandled. The application's pre-check is non-atomic and is bypassed (see the race/rename cases), so it does not enforce the predicate; enforcement is provided by the database. |
| `pc-4e6a6d2471b6` | length: t_st_datasource.datasource_config has length 1023 | `DATABASE_ENFORCED_UNHANDLED` | yes | database | no | unhandled | 0.97 | `ds-control`, `ds-config-len-1024plus` | [t_st_datasource.datasource_config has length 1023] A value of length > 1023 for t_st_datasource.datasource_config passed through the controller/service without any length check and reached the INSERT/UPDATE (SQL trace shows the statement being prepared with the oversized parameter). H2 rejected it with JdbcSQLDataException 22001 'Value too long for column "datasource_config CHARACTER VARYING(1023)"'; GlobalExceptionHandler turned it into HTTP 200 with generic code 99999 'Unknown exception' and leaked the raw SQL/driver message. A boundary value of exactly 1023 characters was accepted where tested. The request map is serialised to JSON by the service before insert; the serialised length (1122) exceeded 1023. |

A boundary value of exactly 63 characters was accepted where tested. Also reproduced on PUT /datasource/{id}. |

| `pc-98f0c2c9584a` | not-null: t_st_datasource.datasource_config must not be null | `APPLICATION_REJECTED` | yes | application | yes | elegant | 0.95 | `ds-config-null`, `ds-config-empty` | [t_st_datasource.datasource_config must not be null] DatasourceServiceImpl.createDatasource rejects an empty/null config map (MapUtils.isEmpty) with SeatunnelException code 60007 'datasource pram [datasourceConfig] not allowed null' before the INSERT (no write in SQL trace). The check is stricter than the predicate (also rejects '{}'). |
| `pc-b04d31c4a9da` | not-null: t_st_datasource.datasource_name must not be null | `DATABASE_ENFORCED_UNHANDLED` | yes | database | no | unhandled | 0.96 | `ds-control`, `ds-name-null` | [t_st_datasource.datasource_name must not be null] Omitting t_st_datasource.datasource_name in the request caused the service to build an INSERT without that column (SQL trace), and H2 raised JdbcSQLIntegrityConstraintViolationException 23502 'NULL not allowed for column "datasource_name"'. The application performed no null check; the failure surfaced as generic code 99999 'Unknown exception' with the raw DB message. On update, a blank name is skipped (r2-ds-update-name-null + read-back keep the old name), so the update path cannot null it. |

Renaming a job to an existing name through PUT /job/config/{versionId} has no application check at all: the UPDATE reached H2, which raised 23505 on CONSTRAINT_INDEX_D ON public.t_st_job_definition(name, workspace_id). Concurrent creates also reached the DB constraint. Both surfaced as generic code 99999 with the raw SQL message. The application's pre-check is non-atomic and is bypassed (see the race/rename cases), so it does not enforce the predicate; enforcement is provided by the database. |
| `pc-e3ea5cfc4aa4` | length: t_st_job_definition.description has length 255 | `DATABASE_ENFORCED_UNHANDLED` | yes | database | no | unhandled | 0.97 | `jd-desc-boundary-255`, `jd-desc-len-256`, `jc-desc-len-256` | [t_st_job_definition.description has length 255] A value of length > 255 for t_st_job_definition.description passed through the controller/service without any length check and reached the INSERT/UPDATE (SQL trace shows the statement being prepared with the oversized parameter). H2 rejected it with JdbcSQLDataException 22001 'Value too long for column "description CHARACTER VARYING(255)"'; GlobalExceptionHandler turned it into HTTP 200 with generic code 99999 'Unknown exception' and leaked the raw SQL/driver message. A boundary value of exactly 255 characters was accepted where tested. |

## Warnings

- Only the H2 schema context (dbctx-04bb0fb56807) was deployed; all MySQL-context targets are VARIANT_NOT_DEPLOYED.
- Compatibility patch 0001 (VirtualTableMapper @Param rename) was required for virtual-table creation; cases prefixed vt- ran before it, vt2-/r2- after it on a fresh in-memory database.
- Spring Boot Hazelcast/Cassandra/etc. auto-configurations were excluded via --spring.autoconfigure.exclude so the app starts without a SeaTunnel engine; job execution/metrics were not exercised.
- Concurrent race cases have overlapping log slices.
- Several application-level 'rejections' are incidental NullPointerExceptions/enum parse errors surfaced as generic code 99999/99998.
- Revision 2: 12 pre-persistence failures caused by incidental exceptions or enum/number binding errors (generic 99999/99998 responses) are reported as INCONCLUSIVE_PRE_PERSISTENCE_FAILURE instead of APPLICATION_REJECTED; the two composite-unique DATABASE_ENFORCED_UNHANDLED results set application_layer_implemented=false because the non-atomic app pre-check is bypassable; APPLICATION_TRANSFORMED results report response_handling=elegant (normal success responses).
